Here's what I found:\n\n## Overview of Penn World Table\n\nThe Penn World Table (PWT) is a set of national-accounts data developed and maintained by scholars at the University of California, Davis and the Groningen Growth Development Centre of the University of Groningen to measure real GDP across countries and over time.\n\n## RGDPNA Variable\n\nRGDPNA (output-side real GDP) quantifies total economic production in volume terms, focusing on value added across sectors and using growth rates from national accounts extrapolated via PPP benchmarks. More specifically, PWT 8.1 provides a variable, \"rgdpna\", which is also based on national accounts growth rates instead of price data.\n\n## Current Data Coverage\n\nThe latest version 11.0 of the Penn World Table contains 47 variables for 185 countries for the period 1950 to 2023. Definition: Real GDP using national-accounts growth rates for studies comparing output-based growth rates across countries\n- Units: Million 2017 USD\n- Calculation: Uses constant 2017 national prices as base; incorporates PPP adjustments and national accounts growth rates\n- Use Case: Comparing growth rates between countries\n\n**K (Capital Stock)**\n- Definition: Measures of capital stock at constant 2017 national prices\n- Units: Million 2017 USD\n- Calculation: Cumulated from series on investment in buildings and different types of machinery; uses constant relative prices for structures and equipment that apply universally across countries\n- Components: Buildings, machinery and equipment, and other capital investments\n- Use Case: Growth accounting, productivity analysis\n\n**L (Labor Input)**\n- Definition: Labor quantity adjusted for educational attainment\n- Units: Millions of persons engaged\n- Components: Number of persons engaged, average annual hours worked\n- Adjustment: Corrected for differences in schooling between countries\n- Use Case: Growth accounting, productivity decomposition\n\n### IMF WEO Definitions:\n\n**Real GDP Growth (NGDP_RPCH)**\n- Annual percentage change in real gross domestic product\n- Based on constant prices\n- Represents volume changes in economic output\n\n**Nominal GDP (NGDPD)**\n- GDP measured at current prices\n- In USD billions\n- Current market values\n\n### ECB Definitions:\n\n**Consumption of Fixed Capital**\n- Definition: Depreciation of produced non-financial assets\n- SNA/ESA Account: P51c\n- Measurement: Amount by which fixed assets are worn out or depleted annually\n- Use Case: Calculating Net Domestic Product, assessing capital replacement needs\n- Valuation: Current prices\n\n---\n\n## SOURCES FOR constant 2017 national prices\n- Units: Million 2017 USD\n- Calculation: Cumulated from series on investment in buildings and different types of machinery; uses constant relative prices for structures and equipment that apply universally across countries\n- Components: Buildings, machinery and equipment, and other capital investments\n- Use Case: Growth accounting, productivity analysis\n\n**L (Labor Input)**\n- Definition: Labor quantity adjusted for educational attainment\n- Units: Millions of persons engaged\n- Components: Number of persons engaged, average annual hours worked\n- Adjustment: Corrected for differences in schooling between countries\n- Use Case: Growth accounting, productivity decomposition\n\n### IMF WEO Definitions:\n\n**Real GDP Growth (NGDP_RPCH)**\n- Annual percentage change in real gross domestic product\n- Based on constant prices\n- Represents volume changes in economic output\n\n**Nominal GDP (NGDPD)**\n- GDP measured at current prices\n- In USD billions\n- Current market values\n\n### ECB Definitions:\n\n**Consumption of Fixed Capital**\n- Definition: Depreciation of produced non-financial assets\n- SNA/ESA Account: P51c\n- Measurement: Amount by which fixed assets are worn out or depleted annually\n- Use Case: Calculating Net Domestic Product, assessing capital replacement needs\n- Valuation: Current prices\n\n---\n\n## SOURCES FOR You have different tools and workflows available for different tasks.\n\n## Important Requirements\n\n**LibreOffice Required for Formula Recalculation**: You can assume LibreOffice is installed for recalculating formula values using the `recalc.py` script. The script automatically configures LibreOffice on first run\n\n## Reading and analyzing data\n\n### Data analysis with pandas\nFor data analysis, visualization, and basic operations, use **pandas** which provides powerful data manipulation capabilities:\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# Read Excel\ndf = pd.read_excel('file.xlsx')  # Default: first sheet\nall_sheets = pd.read_excel('file.xlsx', sheet_name=None)  # All sheets as dict\n\n# Analyze\ndf.head()      # Preview data\ndf.info()      # Column info\ndf.describe()  # Statistics\n\n# Write Excel\ndf.to_excel('output.xlsx', index=False)\n```\n\n## Excel File Workflows\n\n## CRITICAL: Use Formulas, Not Hardcoded Values\n\n**Always use Excel formulas instead of calculating values in Python and hardcoding them.** This ensures the spreadsheet remains dynamic and updateable.\n\n### ❌ WRONG - Hardcoding Calculated Values\n```python\n# Bad: Calculating in Python and hardcoding result\ntotal = df['Sales'].sum()\nsheet['B10'] = total  # Hardcodes 5000\n\n# Bad: Computing growth rate in Python\ngrowth = (df.iloc[-1]['Revenue'] - df.iloc[0]['Revenue']) / df.iloc[0]['Revenue']\nsheet['C5'] = growth  # Hardcodes 0.15\n\n# Bad: Python calculation for average\navg = sum(values) / len(values)\nsheet['D20'] = avg  # Hardcodes 42.5\n```\n\n### ✅ CORRECT - Using Excel Formulas\n```python\n# Good: Let Excel calculate the sum\nsheet['B10'] = '=SUM(B2:B9)'\n\n# Good: Growth rate as Excel formula\nsheet['C5'] = '=(C4-C2)/C2'\n\n# Good: Average using Excel function\nsheet['D20'] = '=AVERAGE(D2:D19)'\n```\n\nThis applies to ALL calculations - totals, percentages, ratios, differences, etc. The spreadsheet should be able to recalculate when source data changes.\n\n## Common Workflow\n1. **Choose tool**: pandas for data, openpyxl for formulas/formatting\n2. **Create/Load**: Create new workbook or load existing file\n3. **Modify**: Add/edit data, formulas, and formatting\n4. **Save**: Write to file\n5. **Recalculate formulas (MANDATORY IF USING FORMULAS)**: Use the recalc.py script\n   ```bash\n   python recalc.py output.xlsx\n   ```\n6. **Verify and fix any errors**:\n   - The script returns JSON with error details\n   - If `status` is `errors_found`, check `error_summary` for specific error types and locations\n   - Fix the identified errors and recalculate again\n   - Common errors to fix:\n     - `#REF!`: Invalid cell references\n     - `#DIV/0!`: Division by zero\n     - `#VALUE!`: Wrong data type in formula\n     - `#NAME?`: Unrecognized formula name\n\n### Creating new Excel files\n\n```python\n# Using openpyxl for formulas and formatting\nfrom openpyxl import Workbook\nfrom openpyxl.styles import Font, PatternFill, Alignment\n\nwb = Workbook()\nsheet = wb.active\n\n# Add data\nsheet['A1'] = 'Hello'\nsheet['B1'] = 'World'\nsheet.append(['Row', 'of', 'data'])\n\n# Add formula\nsheet['B2'] = '=SUM(A1:A10)'\n\n# Formatting\nsheet['A1'].font = Font(bold=True, color='FF0000')\nsheet['A1'].fill = PatternFill('solid', start_color='FFFF00')\nsheet['A1'].alignment = Alignment(horizontal='center')\n\n# Column width\nsheet.column_dimensions['A'].width = 20\n\nwb.save('output.xlsx')\n```\n\n### Editing existing Excel files\n\n```python\n# Using openpyxl to preserve formulas and formatting\nfrom openpyxl import load_workbook\n\n# Load existing file\nwb = load_workbook('existing.xlsx')\nsheet = wb.active  # or wb['SheetName'] for specific sheet\n\n# Working with multiple sheets\nfor sheet_name in wb.sheetnames:\n    sheet = wb[sheet_name]\n    print(f\"Sheet: {sheet_name}\")\n\n# Modify cells\nsheet['A1'] = 'New Value'\nsheet.insert_rows(2)  # Insert row at position 2\nsheet.delete_cols(3)  # Delete column 3\n\n# Add new sheet\nnew_sheet = wb.create_sheet('NewSheet')\nnew_sheet['A1'] = 'Data'\n\nwb.save('modified.xlsx')\n```\n\n## Recalculating formulas\n\nExcel files created or modified by openpyxl contain formulas as strings but not calculated values. Use the provided `recalc.py` script to recalculate formulas:\n\n```bash\npython recalc.py <excel_file> [timeout_seconds]\n```\n\nExample:\n```bash\npython recalc.py output.xlsx 30\n```\n\nThe script:\n- Automatically sets up LibreOffice macro on first run\n- Recalculates all formulas in all sheets\n- Scans ALL cells for Excel errors (#REF!, #DIV/0!, etc.)\n- Returns JSON with detailed error locations and counts\n- Works on both Linux and macOS\n\n## Formula Verification Checklist\n\nQuick checks to ensure formulas work correctly:\n\n### Essential Verification\n- [ ] **Test 2-3 sample references**: Verify they pull correct values before building full model\n- [ ] **Column mapping**: Confirm Excel columns match (e.g., column 64 = BL, not BK)\n- [ ] **Row offset**: Remember Excel rows are 1-indexed (DataFrame row 5 = Excel row 6)\n\n### Common Pitfalls\n- [ ] **NaN handling**: Check for null values with `pd.notna()`\n- [ ] **Far-right columns**: FY data often in columns 50+\n- [ ] **Multiple matches**: Search all occurrences, not just first\n- [ ] **Division by zero**: Check denominators before using `/` in formulas (#DIV/0!)\n- [ ] **Wrong references**: Verify all cell references point to intended cells (#REF!)\n- [ ] **Cross-sheet references**: Use correct format (Sheet1!A1) for linking sheets\n\n### Formula Testing Strategy\n- [ ] **Start small**: Test formulas on 2-3 cells before applying broadly\n- [ ] **Verify dependencies**: Check all cells referenced in formulas exist\n- [ ] **Test edge cases**: Include zero, negative, and very large values\n\n### Interpreting recalc.py Output\nThe script returns JSON with error details:\n```json\n{\n  \"status\": \"success\",           // or \"errors_found\"\n  \"total_errors\": 0, files: Use `read_only=True` for reading or `write_only=True` for writing\n- Formulas are preserved but not evaluated - use recalc.py to update values\n\n### Working with pandas\n- Specify data types K and Y from CFC/WEO\n    cfc_row = row_map[year]\n    production[f'B{row}'] = f'=CFC_data!B{cfc_row}'\n    production[f'C{row}'] = f'=CFC_data!C{cfc_row}'\n    \n    # LnK and LnY\n    production[f'D{row}'] = f'=IF(B{row}>0,LN(B{row}),\"\")'\n    production[f'E{row}'] = f'=IF(C{row}>0,LN(C{row}),\"\")'\n    \n    # LnZ = LnY - 0.3*LnK (capital share = 0.3)\n    production[f'F{row}'] = f'=IF(AND(D{row}<>\"\",E{row}<>\"\"),E{row}-0.3*D{row},\"\")'\n    \n    # LnZ_HP starts as copy of LnZ (will be optimized by Solver)\n    production[f'G{row}'] = f'=F{row}'\n\n# Second differences for LnZ_HP (for HP filter smoothness constraint)\nfor row in range(8, 30):  # Rows 8-29 correspond to years 2002-2023\n    production[f'H{row}'] = f'=G{row}-2*G{row-1}+G{row-2}'\n\n# LnA-Trend sanity check (deviation from trend)\nfor row in range(6, 30):\n    production[f'I{row}'] = f'=F{row}-G{row}'\n\n# HP Filter setup\nproduction['K3'] = 'HP Filter Lambda (penalty on smoothness):'\nproduction['L3'] = 1600\n\n# Objective function for Solver\n# Minimize: sum of squared deviations + lambda * sum of squared second differences\nproduction['P3'] = 'HP Filter Objective:'\nproduction['P5'] = '=SUMPRODUCT(I6:I29,I6:I29)+$L$3*SUMPRODUCT(H8:H29,H8:H29)'\n\n# Extended Production Function (2002-2043)\nfor year in range(2002, 2044):\n    row = 37 + (year - 2002)\n    production[f'A{row}'] = year\n    \n    if year <= 2023:\n        # Use values from above\n        hist_row = 6 + (year - 2000)\n        production[f'B{row}'] = f'=B{hist_row}'\n        production[f'C{row}'] = f'=C{hist_row}'\n        production[f'D{row}'] = f'=D{hist_row}'\n        production[f'E{row}'] = f'=G{hist_row}'  # Use HP filtered LnZ\n    else:\n        # Project forward\n        production[f'C{row}'] = f'=VLOOKUP({year},WEO_Data!$A$4:$B$56,2,FALSE)'\n        production[f'B{row}'] = f'=B{row-1}*(1+$B$3)'  # Grow by depreciation rate\n        production[f'D{row}'] = f'=IF(C{row}>0,B{row}/C{row},\"\")'\n        production[f'E{row}'] = f'=FORECAST(YEAR(DATE({year},1,1)),Production!$A$6:$A$29,Production!$G$6:$G$29)'\n\n    # Change in capital stock (investment in billions to millions)\n    production[f'H{row}'] = f'=G{row}*1000'\n    \n    # Capital stock with shock\n    if row == 37:  # First year 2002\n        production[f'I{row}'] = f'=B{row}+H{row}'\n    else:\n        production[f'I{row}'] = f'=I{row-1}*(1+$B$3)+H{row}'\n    \n    # Output with shock (scales with additional capital)\n    if year <= 2023:\n        production[f'J{row}'] = f'=F{row}*(I{row}/B{row})^0.3'\n    else:\n        production[f'J{row}'] = f'=C{row}*EXP(E{row})*(I{row}/B{row})^0.3'\n    \n    # Output shock (difference from baseline)\n    production[f'K{row}'] = f'=J{row}-F{row}'\n    \n    # Percentage shock\n    production[f'L{row}'] = f'=IF(F{row}>0,K{row}/F{row},\"\")'\n\n# Format Production sheet\nproduction.column_dimensions['A'].width = 10\nfor col in ['B','C','D','E','F','G','H','I','J','K','L','P']:\n    production.column_dimensions[col].width = 14\n\n# Format numbers\nfor row in range(6, 79):\n    for col in ['B','C','D','E','F','G','H','I','J','K','L']:\n        cell = production[f'{col}{row}']\n        if col in ['B','C','F','G','H','I','J','K']:\n            cell.number_format = '#,##0.00'\n        elif col in ['D','E','L']:\n            cell.number_format = '0.0000'\n\nwb.save('/root/test-supply.xlsx')\nprint(\"Updated Production sheet with data and formulas\")\n\nEOF\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":151,"cache_read_input_tokens":43679,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":151,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ac6b79ba-5ea5-4d5c-8902-a31665ef9ade","uuid":"3faa4cd1-4fcb-4c5f-a59c-8ef0474a93af"}
